22Depend on Yahweh to help you when you are in trouble, and he will take care of you. He will never allow anyone to destroy people who are righteous.


## Psalms 55:23 (id: 10019055023)

23God, you will cause people who deceive and kill others to die before they have lived half as long as most people do. But as for me, I will trust you {to protect me}.


## Psalms 56:0 (id: 10019056000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: David wrote this song when the Philistines made him their prisoner in the city of Gath. The tune is “A Faraway Silent Dove.”


## Psalms 56:1 (id: 10019056001)

1O God, men are attacking me, so please help me! They keep fighting me and making me suffer.


## Psalms 56:2 (id: 10019056002)

2Many proud enemies are fighting against me, so there is continually someone attacking me.


## Psalms 56:3 (id: 10019056003)

3But whenever I am afraid, I trust you {to help me}.


## Psalms 56:4 (id: 10019056004)

4I praise you, God, because you have promised {to take care of me}. I trust you to do that, so I am not afraid. I know that mere humans will not be able to harm me!


## Psalms 56:5 (id: 10019056005)

5My enemies are continually trying to interfere in my activities. They keep planning ways to harm me.


## Psalms 56:6 (id: 10019056006)

6Together they secretly watch where I am going because they are looking for an opportunity to kill me.


## Psalms 56:7 (id: 10019056007)

7They should not be able to avoid punishment for the wicked things they are doing. O God, in your anger, please defeat all of our foreign enemies!


## Psalms 56:8 (id: 10019056008)

8You have watched me carefully as I have wandered to this place. Please stay close to me as I have sad experiences here. I know that you have watched me closely as I have had these experiences.


## Psalms 56:9 (id: 10019056009)

9When I cry to God for help, my enemies will stop attacking me. I know that will happen because God favors me.


## Psalms 56:10 (id: 10019056010)

10I praise you, Yahweh my God, because you have promised {to take care of me}.


## Psalms 56:11 (id: 10019056011)

11I trust you to do that, so I am not afraid. I know that mere humans will not be able to harm me!


## Psalms 56:12 (id: 10019056012)

12God, I have vowed {that I will thank you publicly when you help me}, so I will offer a sacrifice to thank you.


## Psalms 56:13 (id: 10019056013)

13I will be able to do that because you will have rescued me. No one will have killed me. Because you protect me, I will be able to live in your presence here in this world where people are alive.


## Psalms 57:0 (id: 10019057000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: David wrote this song when he fled into a cave to escape from Saul. The tune is “Do Not Destroy.”


## Psalms 57:1 (id: 10019057001)

1O God, please help me! I ask you to do that because I depend on you to protect me. I pray that you will protect me just as a mother bird protects her chicks under her wings. I ask you to do that until the troubles I am having are over.


## Psalms 57:2 (id: 10019057002)

2I am crying {for help} to God, who is greater than all other spirit beings. God will complete what he has begun to do for me.


## Psalms 57:3 (id: 10019057003)

3May he act from where he is in heaven to rescue me. Someone is trying to harm me; may God prevent him from doing that. Selah God is kind and loyal to the people who trust him, so may he help me.


## Psalms 57:4 (id: 10019057004)

4Men who are as dangerous as lions are all around me. I have no safe place to sleep far away from those men who are trying to kill me. They say things about me that make other people want to harm me.


## Psalms 57:5 (id: 10019057005)

5May you have the most glory, God, of anyone in heaven or on earth!


## Psalms 57:6 (id: 10019057006)

6My enemies are trying to capture me, as if they had set out a net to trap me. This has distressed me greatly. Yes, they are trying to capture me, as if they had dug a deep pit along the path where I walk, hoping that I would fall into it. But I believe that God will use what they are doing against them, as if they had fallen into that pit themselves. Selah


## Psalms 57:7 (id: 10019057007)

7God, I trust you completely. So {instead of worrying,} I will sing praises to you.


## Psalms 57:8 (id: 10019057008)

8I will do that enthusiastically. I will begin the day by praising you as I play my harp and lyre.


## Psalms 57:9 (id: 10019057009)

9I will sing songs to praise you, Lord, so that people of other nations can find out about you.


## Psalms 57:10 (id: 10019057010)

10You deserve this praise because your kindness and loyalty are much greater than anything on earth, just as the clouds in the sky are much higher than anything on earth.


## Psalms 57:11 (id: 10019057011)

11May you have the most glory, God, of anyone in heaven or on earth!


## Psalms 58:0 (id: 10019058000)

0Here is a note to the worship leader: The tune is “Do Not Destroy.” David wrote this song.


## Psalms 58:1 (id: 10019058001)

1You {judges} should not think that you can ensure justice if you do not speak out {to condemn wickedness}. You are not judging cases fairly, and that shows that you are acting only as humans{, not as God’s representatives}.


## Psalms 58:2 (id: 10019058002)

2In fact, you keep thinking about doing things that are wrong. The decisions that you make encourage people to commit violent crimes.


## Psalms 58:3 (id: 10019058003)

3Wicked people do wrong things and tell lies from the time when they are born.


## Psalms 58:4 (id: 10019058004)

4Wicked people harm other people just as poisonous snakes harm people. They do not pay attention to God’s commandments, as if they were cobras that refused to listen


## Psalms 58:5 (id: 10019058005)

5and so did not respond to the music that snake charmers were playing. Even the most skillful charmer could not get them to respond to music.


## Psalms 58:6 (id: 10019058006)

6Yahweh my God, wicked people are as dangerous as young lions, but destroy them as if you were breaking the large teeth of such lions.


## Psalms 58:7 (id: 10019058007)

7Make them disappear like water that flows away. When they try to harm people as if they were shooting arrows at them, keep them from hurting those people, as if those arrows had no heads!


## Psalms 58:8 (id: 10019058008)

8May they be like a snail that dissolves as it moves along. May they be like a baby that never lives on earth because it dies before its mother gives birth to it.


## Psalms 58:9 (id: 10019058009)

9God will punish and destroy them as powerfully as if a whirlwind were carrying them away. He will do that more quickly than thorns can catch fire and heat a pot, even if the thorns are dry, not fresh.


## Psalms 58:10 (id: 10019058010)

10Righteous people will celebrate when they see God punish wicked people. It will be as if wicked people were an army that God destroyed and righteous people were walking triumphantly through the battlefield afterwards.


## Psalms 58:11 (id: 10019058011)

11Then people will say, “It is true that there is a reward for righteous people! It is true that God judges fairly the people who live on the earth!”


## Psalms 59:0 (id: 10019059000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: David wrote this song when Saul sent men to watch his house so that they could kill him {when he left it}. The tune is “Do Not Destroy.”


## Psalms 59:1 (id: 10019059001)

1My God, please rescue me from my enemies! Protect me from those who want to attack me!


## Psalms 59:2 (id: 10019059002)

2Rescue me from wicked men who are trying to kill me!


## Psalms 59:3 (id: 10019059003)

3Look! They are hiding and waiting for a chance to kill me! Fierce men have gathered to harm me. Yahweh, they are doing that even though I have not done anything that is wrong!


## Psalms 59:4 (id: 10019059004)

4I have not committed any sin, but they are hurrying to get ready to attack me. Please consider my situation and come quickly to help me.


## Psalms 59:5 (id: 10019059005)

5Yahweh, you are the God who commands the heavenly armies. You are the God whom we Israelites worship. So I ask you to take action and punish all the people who do not honor you. Do not act mercifully toward those wicked people who have acted treacherously toward me. Selah


## Psalms 59:6 (id: 10019059006)

6They are like wild dogs that come out in {the cool of} the evening and growl as they roam around this city {looking for scraps of food}.


## Psalms 59:7 (id: 10019059007)

7They make violent threats so loudly that it is as if they are belching. They think, “No one who hears us {will stop us}!”


## Psalms 59:8 (id: 10019059008)

8But you mock them, Yahweh. You scoff at people who do not honor you.


## Psalms 59:9 (id: 10019059009)

9Because you, God, are the one who protects me and makes me strong, I will depend on you.


## Psalms 59:10 (id: 10019059010)

10You, God, are faithfully loving to me, and you will come to help me. You will defeat my enemies so that I can look triumphantly at them.


## Psalms 59:11 (id: 10019059011)

11But do not kill them {right away}. Otherwise, the Israelite people might forget how you punished them. Instead, use your power to make them wander around helplessly, and then destroy them. Lord, you are like a shield that protects us.


## Psalms 59:12 (id: 10019059012)

12The things that they say are sinful. They curse people falsely. May their pride be like a trap that catches them {and keeps them from doing what they are planning}.


## Psalms 59:13 (id: 10019059013)

13Become angry with them and destroy them so that they do not exist anymore. That way people will know that you, God, rule over the Israelite people and the entire world. Selah


## Psalms 59:14 (id: 10019059014)

14They are like wild dogs that come out in {the cool of} the evening and growl as they roam around this city {looking for scraps of food}.


## Psalms 59:15 (id: 10019059015)

15They roam around searching for food. If they do not get enough to eat, they keep roaming around all night.


## Psalms 59:16 (id: 10019059016)

16But as for me, I will sing each morning about how powerful you are. I will sing joyfully about how faithfully loving you are to me. I will sing about how you protect me when I am in danger.


## Psalms 59:17 (id: 10019059017)

17I will praise you, who enables me to be strong, because you, God, protect me and faithfully love me as you promised.


## Psalms 60:0 (id: 10019060000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: The tune is “The Lily.” David wrote this song when he was leading the Israelite army to fight against Aramean armies from Naharaim and Zobah and his general Joab had to leave and fight the Edomites in the Valley of Salt, where he killed 12, 000 of their soldiers. David wanted to testify to the Israelite people so that they would learn from this song {that they should depend on God to help them}.


## Psalms 60:1 (id: 10019060001)

1God, you have stopped helping us Israelites! Because you have become angry with us, our enemies have been able to break through our ranks. Please enable us to fight victoriously again!


## Psalms 60:2 (id: 10019060002)

2It is as if you have caused a great earthquake that has split open the ground. Our army is struggling, but please make it strong again.


## Psalms 60:3 (id: 10019060003)

3You have caused us Israelites to experience great difficulties. It is as if you have made us drink strong wine that is making us fall down as if we were drunk.


## Psalms 60:4 (id: 10019060004)

4But you have raised a battle flag for those who honor you. They can rally around it when their enemies attack them. Selah


## Psalms 60:5 (id: 10019060005)

5That way you will rescue the people whom you love. Please answer my prayer and use your power to save us.


## Psalms 60:6 (id: 10019060006)

6God is holy {and does not lie}, and he has said, “I delight in the territory that I have measured and divided up for you Israelites to live in. That territory includes land on the east side of the Jordan River, where the Valley of Succoth is, and land on the west side of the Jordan River, where Shechem is.


## Psalms 60:7 (id: 10019060007)

7I own the region of Gilead {east of the Jordan River}, and I own the territory of Manasseh {west of the Jordan River}. The mighty soldiers of the tribe of Ephraim {who fight to protect Israel} are like my helmet, and I have given Israel a ruler from the tribe of Judah.


## Psalms 60:8 (id: 10019060008)

8The Moabites are my servants, like slaves who wash my feet. I own the land of Edom; it is as if I have thrown my sandal onto it to show that it belongs to me. And you Philistines might shout your battle cry against me{, but I will defeat you}.”


## Psalms 60:9 (id: 10019060009)

9But I still need someone to lead my army to the land of Edom and conquer their {capital} city that has strong walls around it.


## Psalms 60:10 (id: 10019060010)

10It seems that you, God, have stopped helping us Israelites. It seems that you do not enable our armies to defeat our enemies when they march out to fight them.


## Psalms 60:11 (id: 10019060011)

11Any help that human beings could give us would not be at all sufficient. So we need you to help us to defeat our enemies.


## Psalms 60:12 (id: 10019060012)

12If you, God, help us defeat our enemies, then we will be able to fight very valiantly.


## Psalms 61:0 (id: 10019061000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: A musician playing a stringed instrument should accompany this song. David wrote this song.


## Psalms 61:1 (id: 10019061001)

1O God, I am calling to you for help. Please do what I ask.


## Psalms 61:2 (id: 10019061002)

2I am far from home, and I feel sad, so I am calling out to you {for help}. Please keep me safe, as if you had put me on top of a high rock.


## Psalms 61:3 (id: 10019061003)

3You protect me. You are like a strong tower into which I can go so that my enemies cannot harm me.


## Psalms 61:4 (id: 10019061004)

4I wish I could always be a guest in your home! Keep me as safe as a baby bird is under its mother’s wings. Selah


## Psalms 61:5 (id: 10019061005)

5God, you know that I have solemnly promised to offer sacrifices to acknowledge you, so please help me. You have given me the blessings that belong to people who honor you.


## Psalms 61:6 (id: 10019061006)

6Please allow me, the king of Israel, to live and rule for many years. Please allow my descendants to rule also.


## Psalms 61:7 (id: 10019061007)

7May I reign for a long time as you watch over me. You are faithful, and you keep your promises, so please protect me.


## Psalms 61:8 (id: 10019061008)

8If you do that, I will always sing songs to praise you, and each day I will offer the sacrifices that I promised to give you.


## Psalms 62:0 (id: 10019062000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: Perform in the style of Jeduthun. David wrote this psalm.


## Psalms 62:1 (id: 10019062001)

1God is the one who saves me from my enemies, and I am waiting quietly for him to do that.


## Psalms 62:2 (id: 10019062002)

2He is the only one who can save me. He protects me as if he were a rock I could climb onto in order to be safe. No one would harm me at all.


## Psalms 62:3 (id: 10019062003)

3My enemies are threatening me, but they will not be able to keep doing that. God will destroy all of them, just as a leaning wall falls down and just as people can push a fence onto the ground.


## Psalms 62:4 (id: 10019062004)

4My enemies are plotting together to do one thing. They want to remove me from my important position. They enjoy telling lies. They say things to bless people, but in their inner beings, they are cursing those people. Selah


## Psalms 62:5 (id: 10019062005)

5Because I am expecting God to help me, I will wait quietly for him alone to do that.


## Psalms 62:6 (id: 10019062006)

6He is the only one who can save me. He protects me as if he were a rock I could climb onto in order to be safe. No one would harm me.


## Psalms 62:7 (id: 10019062007)

7God is the one who saves me and honors me. God is like a huge rock to which I can go to be safe. He makes me strong.


## Psalms 62:8 (id: 10019062008)

8You Israelite people should always trust in God. Pray earnestly to him in his presence. He is the one who keeps us safe. Selah


## Psalms 62:9 (id: 10019062009)

9Certainly, ordinary people lack the power to help, and important people are not as powerful as they seem. If you put them all on a scale, it would be as if they weighed less than air.


## Psalms 62:10 (id: 10019062010)

10It is foolish to expect to get rich by cheating other people or by stealing from them. If you become wealthy, do not rely on your money.


## Psalms 62:11 (id: 10019062011)

11God has said very reliably that he is the one who truly has power.


## Psalms 62:12 (id: 10019062012)

12God is also the one who always keeps his promises. He punishes people who do wrong things but rewards people who do good things.


## Psalms 63:0 (id: 10019063000)

0David wrote this song when he was in the desolate area that belongs to the tribe of Judah.


## Psalms 63:1 (id: 10019063001)

1God, you are the God whom I worship. I greatly desire to be with you. It is as if my body aches for you, just as a person in a dry, hot wilderness greatly desires water.


## Psalms 63:2 (id: 10019063002)

2I have worshiped you in your sanctuary and recognized how powerful and glorious you are.


## Psalms 63:3 (id: 10019063003)

3You are always kind to me, as you promised in your covenant. This is worth more to me than even being alive, so I will always praise you.


## Psalms 63:4 (id: 10019063004)

4I will praise you for as long as I am alive. I will lift up my hands and pray to you.


## Psalms 63:5 (id: 10019063005)

5You satisfy me as if I had just eaten a delicious banquet of rich foods. I will praise you very joyfully.


## Psalms 63:6 (id: 10019063006)

6Sometimes I think about you when I am lying in bed. When that happens, I reflect during the night on how wonderful you are.


## Psalms 63:7 (id: 10019063007)

7{I realize that you are wonderful} because you have always helped me. I sing to you joyfully because I am aware that you protect me just as a mother bird protects her chicks under her wings.


## Psalms 63:8 (id: 10019063008)

8I remain completely loyal to you, and you protect me by your power.


## Psalms 63:9 (id: 10019063009)

9Some people are trying to kill me, but they will die and go to the place where dead people are.


## Psalms 63:10 (id: 10019063010)

10They may die in a battle, and then wild dogs will eat their corpses.


## Psalms 63:11 (id: 10019063011)

11But I, the king {of Israel}, will rejoice in what God has done for me. All those who swear oaths in the name of God will praise him because he will have stopped people from saying things that are not true.


## Psalms 64:0 (id: 10019064000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: David wrote this song.


## Psalms 64:1 (id: 10019064001)

1God, please listen to me as I tell you about what worries me. I am afraid of my enemies; please save me from them.


## Psalms 64:2 (id: 10019064002)

2Protect me from what wicked men are planning to do to me. Protect me from that group of men who do evil things.


## Psalms 64:3 (id: 10019064003)

3The hostile things they say are like sharp swords; their cruel words are like arrows.


## Psalms 64:4 (id: 10019064004)

4They are not afraid of anyone. They falsely accuse people who have done nothing wrong. They are like a person who suddenly jumps up from a hiding place and shoots an arrow at someone.


## Psalms 64:5 (id: 10019064005)

5They encourage each other to do the evil things they are planning. They boast about how they have deceived people. They say, “No one will realize what we are doing!”


## Psalms 64:6 (id: 10019064006)

6They carefully plan the wicked things they want to do. They brag, “We have planned this out perfectly!” It is very difficult to know what people are thinking privately.


## Psalms 64:7 (id: 10019064007)

7But God will suddenly judge and punish them, as if he were shooting an arrow at them and wounding them.


## Psalms 64:8 (id: 10019064008)

8The deceitful things they say will get them into trouble. Everyone who sees what happens to them will stay away from them.


## Psalms 64:9 (id: 10019064009)

9People will think about how God has punished them, and they will tell others about it. As a result, people will not dare to be wicked.


## Psalms 64:10 (id: 10019064010)

10People who do what is right will be very happy that Yahweh has punished those wicked people. They will trust Yahweh to protect them. Yes, those who really want to do good things will be glad.


## Psalms 65:0 (id: 10019065000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: David wrote this song.


## Psalms 65:1 (id: 10019065001)

1God, when you come to your sanctuary on Mount Zion, you will find that we have been waiting quietly to praise you and to do what we promised we would do to honor you.


## Psalms 65:2 (id: 10019065002)

2Because you help people when they pray, people everywhere will pray to you.


## Psalms 65:3 (id: 10019065003)

3When we feel very badly because we have done wrong things, you forgive our sins and make it as if we had not committed them.


## Psalms 65:4 (id: 10019065004)

4When you invite people to come and worship you in your sanctuary, that makes them very happy! When we come to your sanctuary, we realize how good and holy you are, and that satisfies us completely.


## Psalms 65:5 (id: 10019065005)

5When we pray to you, you do good and powerful things to help us. You are the God who saves us. Even people who live in very remote places on the earth, on the other side of the oceans, trust in you.


## Psalms 65:6 (id: 10019065006)

6You are so strong, you were able to use your power to set the mountains in their places.


## Psalms 65:7 (id: 10019065007)

7When there is a storm at sea, you can calm it and stop its waves from crashing on the shore. In the same way, you can keep nations from causing trouble.


## Psalms 65:8 (id: 10019065008)

8People who live in very remote places on the earth deeply respect you because of the miracles that you perform. People who live all over the world celebrate what you do.


## Psalms 65:9 (id: 10019065009)

9You send rain to make the soil fertile so that it will produce good crops. It is as if you bring water to the fields in a stream. That is how you prepare the earth to produce abundant crops.


## Psalms 65:10 (id: 10019065010)

10You send rain on the plowed fields, and you fill their furrows with water. You soften the hard clods of soil with showers, and you help young plants to grow in the fields.


## Psalms 65:11 (id: 10019065011)

11When harvest time comes, the fields are glorious with crops, as if you had put a golden crown on them. You provide food for people in great abundance.


## Psalms 65:12 (id: 10019065012)

12There is plenty of food for sheep and goats to eat in the pastures where they graze. So many crops grow on the hills that it is as if the hills were celebrating.


## Psalms 65:13 (id: 10019065013)

13Flocks of sheep and goats cover the meadows like a garment, and there is so much grain in the valleys that the ground is not visible. It is as if the meadows and valleys are singing and shouting joyfully.


## Psalms 66:1 (id: 10019066001)

1This is a note for the worship leader: This is a song, a psalm. Everyone on earth should shout joyfully to praise God!


## Psalms 66:2 (id: 10019066002)

2They should sing songs that tell how glorious God is and how much he deserves praise for being glorious.


## Psalms 66:3 (id: 10019066003)

3Everyone should tell God, “You do such amazing things! You are so powerful that your enemies come and bow down fearfully in front of you.


## Psalms 66:4 (id: 10019066004)

4Everyone on earth should worship you and sing to praise you for your excellent character.” Selah


## Psalms 66:5 (id: 10019066005)

5Come and consider what God has done! Think about the amazing things that he has done among people.


## Psalms 66:6 (id: 10019066006)

6He made a dry path through the Sea of Reeds so that our ancestors could walk right through it. They also walked across the Jordan River {when he stopped it from flowing}. After they crossed, they celebrated what God had done, and we should still celebrate that today.


## Psalms 66:7 (id: 10019066007)

7He is so powerful that he rules forever. He observes what all the people groups do, so anyone who considers rebelling against him should not think that he will be able to do that. Selah


## Psalms 66:8 (id: 10019066008)

8People from every nation, praise our God! Praise him loudly so that others will hear you as you praise him.


## Psalms 66:9 (id: 10019066009)

9He has kept us alive, and he has not allowed anyone to destroy us.


## Psalms 66:10 (id: 10019066010)

10God, you have allowed us to experience great difficulties {in order to make us pure}, just as people put silver in a hot fire so they can purify it.


## Psalms 66:11 (id: 10019066011)

11It is as if you allowed someone to catch us in a trap or made us carry a load that was almost too heavy for us.


## Psalms 66:12 (id: 10019066012)

12It is as if you allowed our enemies to trample on us. We experienced troubles that were like walking through fires and floods. But now you have brought us to a place where we are safe and have plenty of food.


## Psalms 66:13 (id: 10019066013)

13God, I have come to your temple bringing offerings for the priests to burn completely on the altar. I am doing what I promised to do for you.


## Psalms 66:14 (id: 10019066014)

14When I was experiencing great trouble, I said that I would bring these offerings to you {if you rescued me}.


## Psalms 66:15 (id: 10019066015)

15So I have brought fat sheep to burn on the altar, and I have also brought bulls and goats. When they are burning, you will enjoy the smell of the smoke. Selah


## Psalms 66:16 (id: 10019066016)

16All you people who deeply respect God, come and listen as I tell you what he has done for me.


## Psalms 66:17 (id: 10019066017)

17I called out to him to help me, and I praised him as I was praying to him.


## Psalms 66:18 (id: 10019066018)

18If I had secretly intended to commit sins, the Lord would not have done what I asked.


## Psalms 66:19 (id: 10019066019)

19{But I did not do that,} so God has done what I asked.


## Psalms 66:20 (id: 10019066020)

20I praise God because he did what I asked him to do. He has continued to be kind to me, as he promised.


## Psalms 67:0 (id: 10019067000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: This is a song, a psalm. Musicians playing stringed instruments should accompany this song.


## Psalms 67:1 (id: 10019067001)

1May God always be kind to us and bless us. May he consider us favorably. Selah


## Psalms 67:2 (id: 10019067002)

2God, please do that so that all of the people in the world will know how you want them to live and that you are the one who saves them.


## Psalms 67:3 (id: 10019067003)

3May all the people in the world praise you, God!


## Psalms 67:4 (id: 10019067004)

4You judge those nations fairly and show them what they should do. So may they praise you loudly and joyfully! Selah


## Psalms 67:5 (id: 10019067005)

5May all the people in the world praise you, God!


## Psalms 67:6 (id: 10019067006)

6Good crops have grown on our land. May the God whom we Israelites worship continue to give us good harvests.


## Psalms 67:7 (id: 10019067007)

7May God bless us, and may that cause people throughout the world to respect him greatly.


## Psalms 68:0 (id: 10019068000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: David wrote this psalm. It is a song.


## Psalms 68:1 (id: 10019068001)

1God, take action and chase away your enemies! Make those who hate you run away from you.


## Psalms 68:2 (id: 10019068002)

2Chase your enemies away just as wind blows smoke away. Make bad people go away, God, just as wax melts when it is near a fire.


## Psalms 68:3 (id: 10019068003)

3But make good people happy. May they rejoice when they are in your presence; may they be very joyful.


## Psalms 68:4 (id: 10019068004)

4Sing praise to God! Sing a song for him who even cares for desolate regions. His name is Yahweh; be glad when you are in his presence.


## Psalms 68:5 (id: 10019068005)

5God, who is present in his sanctuary, is like a father to those who are orphans, and he protects widows.


## Psalms 68:6 (id: 10019068006)

6He provides families for those who have no one to live with. He frees prisoners and enables them to be successful. But those who rebel against him will not be successful. It will be as if they live in a place where it is very hard to grow crops.


## Psalms 68:7 (id: 10019068007)

7God, you led your people out of Egypt, and then you marched with them through the desert. Selah


## Psalms 68:8 (id: 10019068008)

8After you did that, the earth shook at Mount Sinai when you appeared to your people. Rain poured down from the sky, and your people worshiped you.


## Psalms 68:9 (id: 10019068009)

9You caused much rain to fall there in the wilderness, and so you enabled good crops to grow again on that land that you gave to us Israelites.


## Psalms 68:10 (id: 10019068010)

10Your people built homes there. You were kind to poor people and provided food for them.


## Psalms 68:11-12 (id: 10019068011)

11\-12Kings led their armies to fight against the Israelites, but God defeated them and they ran away. The Lord declared victory over them, and a great crowd of Israelite women sang and danced to celebrate. The men who fought against these enemies captured many valuable possessions and brought them back to the women who were at home.


## Psalms 68:13 (id: 10019068013)

13Some men had to take care of the sheep {instead of fighting}, but the soldiers captured enough valuable possessions to give them some as well. They received treasures such as a dove statue with silver wings and feathers shimmering with fine gold.


## Psalms 68:14 (id: 10019068014)

14Shaddai defeated the enemy kings and their armies by making it snow on Mount Zalmon!


## Psalms 68:15 (id: 10019068015)

15Mount Bashan is a very high mountain. It has many peaks.


## Psalms 68:16 (id: 10019068016)

16Because it is such a great mountain, if it could think, it might think that God should have chosen it as the place for his sanctuary. But Yahweh chose Mount Zion instead, and he will always be present there.


## Psalms 68:17 (id: 10019068017)

17God is so powerful that it is as if he has thousands and thousands of chariots. In fact, it is as if he has ten thousands and ten thousands of them! The same God who appeared to the Israelites on Mount Sinai is now present in his sanctuary on Mount Zion.


## Psalms 68:18 (id: 10019068018)

18{After he defeated his enemies,} he went up to the sacred mountain where his sanctuary is. He led many captured prisoners in a victory procession. He received gifts from the enemies whom he had defeated. He even received gifts from those who had rebelled against him. Now Yahweh, our God, will always be present in his sanctuary.


## Psalms 68:19 (id: 10019068019)

19Praise the Lord, who helps us every day as if he were carrying a heavy load for us. He is the God who saves us. Selah


## Psalms 68:20 (id: 10019068020)

20Our God is the God who saves us; Yahweh our Lord is the one who rescues us from dying.


## Psalms 68:21 (id: 10019068021)

21But God will completely destroy his enemies. If anyone continues to behave sinfully, God will destroy that person.


## Psalms 68:22 (id: 10019068022)

22The Lord said, “Even if my enemies try to climb a high mountain to be safe, or even if they try to hide at the bottom of the ocean, I will bring them back


## Psalms 68:23 (id: 10019068023)

23so that you can trample on their heads. Your dogs will lick up the blood that comes out of their heads.”


## Psalms 68:24 (id: 10019068024)

24And now you are parading triumphantly into your sanctuary, God. You are our king, and you are leading a great crowd the way a king does.


## Psalms 68:25 (id: 10019068025)

25There are singers at the front of the parade. Musicians playing stringed instruments are at the back of the parade. In the middle there are girls playing tambourines.


## Psalms 68:26 (id: 10019068026)

26Praise God, you who have gathered together! Praise Yahweh, you descendants of Israel!


## Psalms 68:27 (id: 10019068027)

27People from the tribe of Benjamin, the smallest tribe, are marching first. Leaders and many other people from the tribe of Judah come next. There are even leaders from the {far northern} tribes of Zebulun and Naphtali in the parade.


## Psalms 68:28 (id: 10019068028)

28Our God has made our Israelite tribes strong. God, continue to help us with your power as you helped us in the past.


## Psalms 68:29 (id: 10019068029)

29Since you have now established your sanctuary on a mountain above Jerusalem, kings will send tribute to you there.


## Psalms 68:30 (id: 10019068030)

30Show the king of Egypt that even though he has a powerful army, he cannot defeat you. Many kings who are strong like bulls may gather together. They may form armies from their subject nations, which are like bulls’ calves, {but they will not be able to defeat you either}. Instead, they will come submissively and pay you tribute in silver. Some nations are eager to fight wars; make their armies run away!


## Psalms 68:31 (id: 10019068031)

31Then envoys will come from Egypt {bringing gifts to you}. The ruler of Cush will hasten to send tribute to God.


## Psalms 68:32 (id: 10019068032)

32People of nations throughout the world, sing praise to God! He is the Lord Selah


## Psalms 68:33 (id: 10019068033)

33who dwells above the sky that he created long ago. When you hear thunder, it is as if he is shouting with his very powerful voice.


## Psalms 68:34 (id: 10019068034)

34Proclaim how powerful God is! He is the king who reigns over Israel as he rules powerfully from the sky.


## Psalms 68:35 (id: 10019068035)

35God, you are in your sanctuary, and you are very powerful. This is the God whom we Israelite people worship. He makes us very strong. Praise God!


## Psalms 69:0 (id: 10019069000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: David wrote this song. The tune is “Lilies.”


## Psalms 69:1 (id: 10019069001)

1Please rescue me, God! I am in great danger, as if I were just about to drown in a flood.


## Psalms 69:2 (id: 10019069002)

2It is as if I am sinking in deep mud where there is no solid ground for me to stand on. It is as if I am in deep water and a flood is swirling around me.


## Psalms 69:3 (id: 10019069003)

3I am tired and my throat is dry because I have been calling to you for help for so long. I have been looking for so long for you to come and help me that I have worn out my eyes.


## Psalms 69:4 (id: 10019069004)

4There are people who hate me for no reason. There are so many of these people, it is as if there are more of them than there are hairs on my head! The enemies who want to harm me are powerful, and they are telling lies about me. They are demanding that I pay back things that I did not steal!


## Psalms 69:5 (id: 10019069005)

5O God, you know that I have foolishly disobeyed your laws; you are aware of the sins that I have committed.


## Psalms 69:6 (id: 10019069006)

6But Yahweh my Lord, commander of the heavenly armies, may the wrong things I have done not disappoint the people who trust you. You are the God whom we Israelite people should worship. Please do not let me embarrass those who do worship you.


## Psalms 69:7 (id: 10019069007)

7People have insulted me because I am loyal to you. They have thoroughly humiliated me.


## Psalms 69:8 (id: 10019069008)

8Even my own family members act as if they do not know me.


## Psalms 69:9 (id: 10019069009)

9I care so much about your temple that people are causing trouble for me. People are speaking disrespectfully of you, and it is as if they are also insulting me.


## Psalms 69:10 (id: 10019069010)

10I wept and fasted very sincerely, but they mocked me for doing that.


## Psalms 69:11 (id: 10019069011)

11I wore rough clothing {to show that I was sad}, but they used me as an example of a foolish person.


## Psalms 69:12 (id: 10019069012)

12Even the elders of the city say bad things about me. The drunkards in the city sing insulting songs about me.


## Psalms 69:13 (id: 10019069013)

13But I will continue praying to you, Yahweh. You are always very kind to me, as you have promised. So at the time that you choose, please answer my prayer by rescuing me, since you love me faithfully.


## Psalms 69:14 (id: 10019069014)

14Rescue me so that it will no longer be as if I were sinking in mud. Rescue me from my enemies so that it will no longer be as if I were drowning.


## Psalms 69:15 (id: 10019069015)

15Make it so that it will no longer be as if a flood were swirling around me and I was about to go down to the bottom of deep water. Rescue me so that I do not go to the place where dead people are.


## Psalms 69:16 (id: 10019069016)

16Yahweh, it is so good that you faithfully love me, so please do what I ask. Because you are very merciful, please help me.


## Psalms 69:17 (id: 10019069017)

17I am experiencing much trouble, so please consider my situation and act quickly to help me.


## Psalms 69:18 (id: 10019069018)

18Come and save me; please rescue me from my enemies.


## Psalms 69:19 (id: 10019069019)

19You know personally how people are insulting, shaming, and dishonoring me. You know who all my enemies are.


## Psalms 69:20 (id: 10019069020)

20Their insults have deeply offended me, and I feel very badly. I wanted someone to feel sorry for me and encourage me, but no one did.


## Psalms 69:21 (id: 10019069021)

21Instead, they put poison in my food, and when I was thirsty, they gave me sour wine to drink.


## Psalms 69:22 (id: 10019069022)

22May their own food be like a trap that they walk into! May it harm their health.


## Psalms 69:23 (id: 10019069023)

23May their eyes become dim so that they cannot see anything. May their bodies keep getting weaker.


## Psalms 69:24 (id: 10019069024)

24Become angry with them and punish them! Angrily pursue them until you catch them.


## Psalms 69:25 (id: 10019069025)

25Make them abandon their towns so that no one lives in them anymore.


## Psalms 69:26 (id: 10019069026)

26Do this because they did things to harm people whom you were already disciplining. They said that people whom you were correcting were suffering {because they had done bad things}.


## Psalms 69:27 (id: 10019069027)

27Punish them for every wrong thing they have done. Do not forgive them!


## Psalms 69:28 (id: 10019069028)

28Remove their names from the book that records the names of people who have everlasting life; do not include them in your list of righteous people.


## Psalms 69:29 (id: 10019069029)

29As for me, I am in bad circumstances, and I am suffering. O God, please protect me and save me.


## Psalms 69:30 (id: 10019069030)

30When God does that, I will sing a song to praise God, and I will honor him by thanking him.


## Psalms 69:31 (id: 10019069031)

31When I do that, it will please Yahweh more than if I had sacrificed oxen, yes, he will like that better than if I had offered him full\-grown bulls.


## Psalms 69:32 (id: 10019069032)

32Humble people will be happy when they see {that God has rescued me}. This will encourage people who worship God sincerely.


## Psalms 69:33 (id: 10019069033)

33When needy people pray to Yahweh, he does what they ask. He greatly respects people who suffer for him.


## Psalms 69:34 (id: 10019069034)

34May the entire creation praise God: the birds in the sky, the animals on the land, and the creatures in the sea.


## Psalms 69:35 (id: 10019069035)

35May God protect the people who live in Jerusalem and in other places in Judah. May he strengthen their cities so that they keep living in them as their territory.


## Psalms 69:36 (id: 10019069036)

36May the descendants of the people who are loyal to him inherit that territory, and may those who love him live there safely.


## Psalms 70:0 (id: 10019070000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: David wrote this psalm to remind {God that he had promised to help him}.


## Psalms 70:1 (id: 10019070001)

1Yahweh my God, please act quickly to rescue me!


## Psalms 70:2 (id: 10019070002)

2Some people are trying to kill me. Embarrass them by making them fail to do that. Some people would be happy to harm me. Humiliate them by making them abandon that plan.


## Psalms 70:3 (id: 10019070003)

3Some people are saying that I am suffering because I am a bad person. Make them ashamed of saying that so that they stop saying it.


## Psalms 70:4 (id: 10019070004)

4{Rescue me so that} everyone who is loyal to you will greatly celebrate. Then the people who depend on you to rescue them will keep saying, “God certainly is great!”


## Psalms 70:5 (id: 10019070005)

5But right now I am in very difficult circumstances, so please act quickly to help me, God! Yahweh, you are the one who helps me and saves me, so please help me right away!


## Psalms 71:1 (id: 10019071001)

1You, Yahweh, are the one who keeps me safe. May it always be clear that I was right to trust you.


## Psalms 71:2 (id: 10019071002)

2Because you always do what is right, take action to help me. Consider my situation and save me!


## Psalms 71:3 (id: 10019071003)

3You declare that I will be safe, since you are the one who keeps me safe and protects me, so please be like a huge rock on top of which I can always go to be safe.


## Psalms 71:4 (id: 10019071004)

4My God, please rescue me from what wicked people want to do to me. Rescue me from the power of violent, evil men.


## Psalms 71:5 (id: 10019071005)

5Yahweh my Lord, you are the one whom I confidently expect to help me. I have trusted in you ever since I was young.


## Psalms 71:6 (id: 10019071006)

6I have depended on you for my entire life; you even made sure that I was born safely, so I will always praise you.


## Psalms 71:7 (id: 10019071007)

7It shocks many people to see me now, but you are my mighty protector.


## Psalms 71:8 (id: 10019071008)

8I praise you lavishly throughout the day for how glorious you are.


## Psalms 71:9 (id: 10019071009)

9Please keep helping me now that I have become an old man. Please help me even though I am not strong anymore.


## Psalms 71:10 (id: 10019071010)

10My enemies are saying that they want to kill me, and they are planning together how to do that.


## Psalms 71:11 (id: 10019071011)

11They are saying, “No one will rescue him, since God is not helping him anymore, so we can pursue him and capture him.”


## Psalms 71:12 (id: 10019071012)

12My God, do not neglect me; hurry to help me!


## Psalms 71:13 (id: 10019071013)

13Embarrass the people who want to kill me by making their plans fail. Yes, completely shame and disgrace those who want to harm me!


## Psalms 71:14 (id: 10019071014)

14But as for me, I will always trust you, and I will praise you even more {now than I have in the past}.


## Psalms 71:15 (id: 10019071015)

15I will tell people how you have saved me because you are good. I will do that continually because there will always be more for me to say about this.


## Psalms 71:16 (id: 10019071016)

16Yahweh my Lord, you are the one who gives me great strength. I will recall {to others} how you do what is right in a way that no one else does.


## Psalms 71:17 (id: 10019071017)

17God, you have taught me many things ever since I was young, and I still tell people today about the wonderful things you do.


## Psalms 71:18 (id: 10019071018)

18God, please keep helping me now that I am old and my hair is gray. That way I will be able to tell my descendants how powerfully you have helped me.


## Psalms 71:19 (id: 10019071019)

19God, you do so many great things because you are good, it is as if they have piled up to the sky. There is no one else like you, God!


## Psalms 71:20 (id: 10019071020)

20You have allowed me to experience many very difficult things, and it has seemed as if I was about to go to the place where dead people are, but now please give me a good life again.


## Psalms 71:21 (id: 10019071021)

21Please make me even greater than I was before, and comfort me rather than allow me to have difficult experiences.


## Psalms 71:22 (id: 10019071022)

22When you do that, then I will play my lute and praise you, my God, for faithfully helping me. I will play my harp as I sing to praise you. You are the holy God whom we Israelites worship.


## Psalms 71:23 (id: 10019071023)

23I will shout joyfully while I sing. Because you will have rescued me, I will sing to you wholeheartedly.


## Psalms 71:24 (id: 10019071024)

24You will have defeated and disgraced the people who wanted to harm me, so throughout the day I will describe how you did what was right.


## Psalms 72:1 (id: 10019072001)

1A psalm of Solomon. O God, please enable the man who is now succeeding his father as king to judge cases rightly, as you would do.


## Psalms 72:2 (id: 10019072002)

2Enable him to judge the cases of Israelite people fairly, especially for people who are vulnerable.


## Psalms 72:3 (id: 10019072003)

3May the people live rightly so that they will be prosperous as crops grow abundantly on the hillsides of the land.


## Psalms 72:4 (id: 10019072004)

4Help the king to defend poor people and rescue needy people and defeat those who are oppressing them.


## Psalms 72:5 (id: 10019072005)

5May each successive generation of the people respect you. May they do that for as long as the sun and the moon are in the sky!


## Psalms 72:6 (id: 10019072006)

6May this king make the nation prosperous, just as heavy rains soak the soil and enable more hay to grow after a first cutting.


## Psalms 72:7 (id: 10019072007)

7May people who live rightly prosper during the time when he reigns, and may the nation be very prosperous for as long as the moon is in the sky.


## Psalms 72:8 (id: 10019072008)

8May he rule over all of the surrounding territory, right up to the shores of the seas. May his realm extend from the Euphrates River to the limits of the lands that people know.


## Psalms 72:9 (id: 10019072009)

9May the leaders of the desert tribes bow down in front of him {to acknowledge him as their ruler}, and may his enemies lie facedown on the ground {in submission to him}.


## Psalms 72:10 (id: 10019072010)

10May the kings who rule the land of Tarshish and other coastal areas pay tribute to him. May the king of Sheba and the king of Seba give him gifts.


## Psalms 72:11 (id: 10019072011)

11In fact, may all the kings in the world acknowledge him as their ruler, and may every nation obey him.


## Psalms 72:12 (id: 10019072012)

12May all these things happen because he rescues needy people when they cry out for help and he helps poor people who have no one else to help them.


## Psalms 72:13 (id: 10019072013)

13May he feel compassion for people who are weak and needy and save their lives.


## Psalms 72:14 (id: 10019072014)

14May their lives be important to him so that he rescues them from people who are oppressing and harming them.


## Psalms 72:15 (id: 10019072015)

15May this king live for a long time! May he receive much fine gold. May people always pray for him and ask God to bless him.


## Psalms 72:16 (id: 10019072016)

16May the fields produce such abundant crops that grain grows all the way up to the tops of the hills and sways in the wind like the branches of the trees in Lebanon. May the cities in Israel be as full of people as the fields are full of grass.


## Psalms 72:17 (id: 10019072017)

17May people always remember what a good king this was, for as long as the sun is in the sky! May he do good things for many people, and may the people of other nations say that he has a good life.


## Psalms 72:18 (id: 10019072018)

18Praise Yahweh, the God whom we Israelites worship; he does amazing things that no one else can do.


## Psalms 72:19 (id: 10019072019)

19Praise him forever for how glorious he is! May his glory fill the whole world! Amen! Yes, amen!


## Psalms 72:20 (id: 10019072020)

20This is the end of the prayers that David, the son of Jesse, wrote.


## Psalms 73:1 (id: 10019073001)

1Asaph wrote this psalm. God truly is good to us Israelite people, to those who want to do what pleases God.


## Psalms 73:2 (id: 10019073002)

2As for me, I almost stopped trusting in God; I was almost guilty of committing a great sin against him


## Psalms 73:3 (id: 10019073003)

3because I envied those who proudly acted as if they did not need God, when I realized that they prospered even though they were wicked.


## Psalms 73:4 (id: 10019073004)

4Those people do not suffer; they always have plenty of food to eat.


## Psalms 73:5 (id: 10019073005)

5They do not experience the difficult things that other people do; they do not experience catastrophes and sicknesses like other people.


## Psalms 73:6 (id: 10019073006)

6So they are proud and violent. Their being prideful is like a necklace that they show off to others, and they openly display their violent actions like people openly display the clothes they wear.


## Psalms 73:7 (id: 10019073007)

7Their eyes protrude because their faces are fat, and they are always thinking about more evil things to do.


## Psalms 73:8 (id: 10019073008)

8They scoff at other people, and wickedly talk about doing unjust things to them; they speak pridefully.


## Psalms 73:9 (id: 10019073009)

9They exalt themselves and talk as if they are God in heaven, and they go about the earth talking boastfully.


## Psalms 73:10 (id: 10019073010)

10The result is that people pay attention to them and listen to everything they say.


## Psalms 73:11 (id: 10019073011)

11Wicked people say to themselves, “God certainly does not care what we do; the Most High certainly will not judge us for what we do!”


## Psalms 73:12 (id: 10019073012)

12Look at how these wicked people live; their lives are so easy that they never have to worry about anything, and they are always becoming richer.


## Psalms 73:13 (id: 10019073013)

13{When I saw this I thought that} certainly it is useless that I have thought about things that are pleasing to you and have not sinned.


## Psalms 73:14 (id: 10019073014)

14Every day I suffer, and every morning you punish me.


## Psalms 73:15 (id: 10019073015)

15If I had said these things out loud in front of others, surely I would have been sinning against your people.


## Psalms 73:16 (id: 10019073016)

16When I tried to think about all this, it was very difficult for me to understand it.


## Psalms 73:17 (id: 10019073017)

17But when I went to the temple where people worship God, then I understood what will happen to wicked people in the future.


## Psalms 73:18 (id: 10019073018)

18I know that you make their future insecure; you will cause them to be ruined.


## Psalms 73:19 (id: 10019073019)

19God will suddenly destroy these wicked people; they will die in terrible ways.


## Psalms 73:20 (id: 10019073020)

20Lord, when you act, you will cause the wicked to quickly vanish; they will vanish like a dream which disappears as soon as a person awakes from their sleep.


## Psalms 73:21 (id: 10019073021)

21When I was grieved in my inner being and felt emotionally hurt,


## Psalms 73:22 (id: 10019073022)

22I was stupid and ignorant; I behaved like an animal toward you.


## Psalms 73:23 (id: 10019073023)

23Nevertheless I am always with you; you constantly stay close to me.


## Psalms 73:24 (id: 10019073024)

24You lead me by instructing me, and when I die, you will take me into your glorious presence.


## Psalms 73:25 (id: 10019073025)

25You are the only one I have in heaven! There is nothing on this earth that I desire more than you.


## Psalms 73:26 (id: 10019073026)

26My body and my spirit may become very weak, but God enables me to feel encouraged; he has granted to me that I would belong to him forever.


## Psalms 73:27 (id: 10019073027)

27People who abandon you will perish; you will ruin every person who is not loyal to you.


## Psalms 73:28 (id: 10019073028)

28But for me, the only thing I need is to be close to God. I trust in the Lord Yahweh to keep me safe so that I may tell others everything you have done for me.


## Psalms 74:1 (id: 10019074001)

1A Levite from the clan of Asaph wrote this song. God, it feels as if you have permanently abandoned us! You care for us Israelites the way a shepherd cares for his flock. So please do not continue to be angry with us.


## Psalms 74:2 (id: 10019074002)

2Think about your people whom you chose long ago. You rescued us from slavery so that we would belong to you. Think about Mount Zion, where you have been present {in your temple}.


## Psalms 74:3 (id: 10019074003)

3Come and walk through the ruins of your temple. It seems that no one will ever rebuild it. Look at all the damage that the enemy soldiers did to your temple.


## Psalms 74:4 (id: 10019074004)

4Your enemies shouted in triumph right there in the place where you met with us Israelites. They set up their battle flags to show that they had conquered it.


## Psalms 74:5 (id: 10019074005)

5They acted like woodsmen who were swinging axes to cut down trees in a forest.


## Psalms 74:6 (id: 10019074006)

6They smashed all the carved wooden panels with their axes and hammers.


## Psalms 74:7 (id: 10019074007)

7Then they burned down your temple completely. They treated the temple where you were present as if it were not a sacred place.


## Psalms 74:8 (id: 10019074008)

8They said to themselves, “We will completely subdue the Israelites.” They burned down every place in the land where people gathered to worship God.


## Psalms 74:9 (id: 10019074009)

9You no longer give us any signs, and there are no prophets anymore. None of us knows how long this will last.


## Psalms 74:10 (id: 10019074010)

10God, do not allow your opponents to continue to insult you! Do not allow your enemies to keep treating you with disrespect anymore!


## Psalms 74:11 (id: 10019074011)

11You should not fail to use your great power. Use your power to destroy your enemies!


## Psalms 74:12 (id: 10019074012)

12God, I acknowledge that you have ruled from ancient times. You have been saving people throughout the earth.


## Psalms 74:13 (id: 10019074013)

13By your great power, you pushed back the sea so that the dry land would appear. You created order out of the watery chaos.


## Psalms 74:14 (id: 10019074014)

14It was as if you smashed the heads of a chaos monster that lived in the sea and left his body in the desert for scavengers to eat.


## Psalms 74:15 (id: 10019074015)

15You are able to make springs and streams flow in dry areas, but you are also able to dry up rivers that usually flow all the time.


## Psalms 74:16 (id: 10019074016)

16You determine when it becomes day and when it becomes night. You make it light during the day when the sun shines.


## Psalms 74:17 (id: 10019074017)

17You are the one who set all the boundaries of the earth. You are the one who created the seasons of summer and winter.


## Psalms 74:18 (id: 10019074018)

18Yahweh, remember that your enemies have insulted you and that foolish people have mocked your reputation.


## Psalms 74:19 (id: 10019074019)

19Do not allow cruel enemies to destroy the people whom you cherish. Save the lives of your vulnerable people before it is too late.


## Psalms 74:20 (id: 10019074020)

20People are committing violent acts in places where they think others will not see them. So we need you to help us. Please do that because of the covenant that you made with us.


## Psalms 74:21 (id: 10019074021)

21We are asking you to help us; please do not disappoint us. Then we poor and needy people will praise you for what you have done.


## Psalms 74:22 (id: 10019074022)

22Take action to defend yourself, God! Do not allow foolish people to keep insulting you!


## Psalms 74:23 (id: 10019074023)

23Do not ignore what your enemies are saying. Your opponents keep shouting things against you.


## Psalms 75:0 (id: 10019075000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: Asaph wrote this song. The tune is “Do Not Destroy.”


## Psalms 75:1 (id: 10019075001)

1We thank you, God! We thank you because you are close {to us}. We tell people about the wonderful things that you have done.


## Psalms 75:2 (id: 10019075002)

2{You say,} “I have set a time {to judge people}. At that time, I will judge everyone fairly.


## Psalms 75:3 (id: 10019075003)

3The earth may shake, and everyone who lives on it may tremble. But I am the one who holds it steady.” Selah


## Psalms 75:4 (id: 10019075004)

4I warn those who boast, “Stop boasting!” I tell wicked people, “Do not be proud!


## Psalms 75:5 (id: 10019075005)

5Do not brag about your power! Do not speak arrogantly!”


## Psalms 75:6 (id: 10019075006)

6{The one who judges and} honors people does not come from the east, west, or south.


## Psalms 75:7 (id: 10019075007)

7God is the one who judges people; he shames and punishes some people, and he honors other people.


## Psalms 75:8 (id: 10019075008)

8It is as if Yahweh holds a cup {full of punishment} in his hand. It is full of {punishment that is like} foaming wine mixed with spices. He pours it out {to punish the wicked}, and all the wicked people on earth must drink it. {He punishes them fully like} they were drinking the cup to the last drop.


## Psalms 75:9 (id: 10019075009)

9But as for me, I will never stop saying what the God whom Jacob worshiped has done; I will never stop singing to praise him.


## Psalms 75:10 (id: 10019075010)

10God promises this: “I will destroy the power of wicked people, but I will increase the power of righteous people.”


## Psalms 76:0 (id: 10019076000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: to be accompanied by stringed instruments. Asaph wrote this psalm.


## Psalms 76:1 (id: 10019076001)

1People in Judah know God. He is famous in Israel.


## Psalms 76:2 (id: 10019076002)

2His home is in Jerusalem. He lives on Mount Zion.


## Psalms 76:3 (id: 10019076003)

3There he shattered the flaming arrows. He destroyed the shields, the swords, and the weapons of war. Selah


## Psalms 76:4 (id: 10019076004)

4You are glorious and excellent. You are more majestic than the mountains where you destroyed your enemies.


## Psalms 76:5 (id: 10019076005)

5Enemy soldiers plundered the brave soldiers. They have fallen into their final sleep. None of the warriors were able to lift their hands {to defend themselves}.


## Psalms 76:6 (id: 10019076006)

6When you, the God whom Jacob worshiped, shouted at them, their war horses and their riders fell down dead.


## Psalms 76:7 (id: 10019076007)

7You are the one whom everyone must fear. No one can survive in your presence when you are angry.


## Psalms 76:8 (id: 10019076008)

8You gave your decision from heaven. The whole world was scared and silent.


## Psalms 76:9 (id: 10019076009)

9This happened when you stood up to judge, O God, to rescue all the oppressed people of the world. Selah


## Psalms 76:10 (id: 10019076010)

10Even human anger will bring you praise. You will use the rest of their anger as a weapon.


## Psalms 76:11 (id: 10019076011)

11Make promises to Yahweh your God and keep them. Let everyone around him bring gifts to the Awesome One.


## Psalms 76:12 (id: 10019076012)

12He humbles the leaders, and terrifies all the kings.


## Psalms 77:0 (id: 10019077000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: Perform in the style of Jeduthun. Asaph wrote this song.


## Psalms 77:1 (id: 10019077001)

1I cried out to God; I cried aloud to him, and he listened to me.


## Psalms 77:2 (id: 10019077002)

2When I was in trouble, I prayed to the Lord; all during the night I lifted up my hands {while I prayed}, and I did not stop {praying}. But nothing could comfort me.


## Psalms 77:3 (id: 10019077003)

3When I thought about God, I despaired; as I thoughtfully considered {him}, I became weak and discouraged. Selah


## Psalms 77:4 (id: 10019077004)

4All during the night he prevented me from sleeping; I was so worried that I did not know what to say.


## Psalms 77:5 (id: 10019077005)

5I thought about days that had passed; I remembered what had happened in previous years.


## Psalms 77:6 (id: 10019077006)

6During the night, I remembered the songs that I used to sing; I thoughtfully considered, and I pondered these things:


## Psalms 77:7 (id: 10019077007)

7{I feared that} the Lord would reject {me} forever and that he would never be kind {to me} again.


## Psalms 77:8 (id: 10019077008)

8{I wondered whether} {the Lord} had completely stopped being faithful and had broken his promise to all future generations.


## Psalms 77:9 (id: 10019077009)

9{I asked myself whether} God had forgotten to be gracious {and whether} his anger had caused him to stop being compassionate. Selah


## Psalms 77:10 (id: 10019077010)

10I said, “What causes me to be sad the most is that it seems that God, who is greater than any other spirit being, is no longer using his power for us.”


## Psalms 77:11 (id: 10019077011)

11But then, Yahweh, I recall your great deeds; I remember the wonderful things that you did in the past.


## Psalms 77:12 (id: 10019077012)

12I contemplated all that you have done, and I thought carefully about your mighty acts.


## Psalms 77:13 (id: 10019077013)

13God, everything that you do is amazing; there is certainly no god who is great like you are!


## Psalms 77:14 (id: 10019077014)

14You are God, the one who performs miracles; you showed to the people of many people groups that you are powerful.


## Psalms 77:15 (id: 10019077015)

15By your power you rescued your people {from Egypt}; you saved those who were descendants of Jacob and his son Joseph. Selah


## Psalms 77:16 (id: 10019077016)

16It was as though the waters saw you and became very afraid, and even the deepest part of the water shook.


## Psalms 77:17 (id: 10019077017)

17Rain poured down from the clouds; it thundered very loudly, and lightning flashed in all directions.


## Psalms 77:18 (id: 10019077018)

18Thunder crashed in the whirlwind—your voice! Lightning lit up, and the earth shook violently.


## Psalms 77:19 (id: 10019077019)

19Then you walked through the sea in a path that you made through the deep water, but your footprints could not be seen.


## Psalms 77:20 (id: 10019077020)

20You led your people as a shepherd leads his flock of sheep while Moses and Aaron were the leaders of your people.


## Psalms 78:1 (id: 10019078001)

1A song of Asaph. My friends, listen to what I am going to teach you; pay careful attention to what I will say.


## Psalms 78:2 (id: 10019078002)

2I am going to give you some sayings that wise people have said. They will be sayings about things that happened long ago, things that were difficult to understand.


## Psalms 78:3 (id: 10019078003)

3These are things that we have heard and known previously, things that our parents and grandparents told us.


## Psalms 78:4 (id: 10019078004)

4We will tell these things to our children, but we will also tell our grandchildren to praise Yahweh for his power and the glorious things that he has done.


## Psalms 78:5 (id: 10019078005)

5He gave requirements and instructions to the Israelites, those who are descended from Jacob, and he told our ancestors to teach them to their children.


## Psalms 78:6 (id: 10019078006)

6He commanded this so that their children would also know them and so that they would teach them to their own children.


## Psalms 78:7 (id: 10019078007)

7In that way, they also would trust in God and would not forget the things that he has done; instead, they would obey his commandments.


## Psalms 78:8 (id: 10019078008)

8They would not be like their ancestors, who were very stubborn and kept rebelling against God; they did not continue firmly trusting in God, and they did not worship him alone.


## Psalms 78:9 (id: 10019078009)

9The soldiers of the tribe of Ephraim had bows and arrows, but they ran away from their enemies on the day that they fought with their enemies.


## Psalms 78:10 (id: 10019078010)

10They did not do what they had agreed with God that they would do; they refused to obey his laws.


## Psalms 78:11 (id: 10019078011)

11They forgot what he had done; they forgot about the miracles that they had seen him perform.


## Psalms 78:12 (id: 10019078012)

12While our ancestors were watching, God performed miracles in the area around the city of Zoan in Egypt.


## Psalms 78:13 (id: 10019078013)

13Then he caused the Sea of Reeds to divide, causing the water on each side to pile up like a wall, with the result that our ancestors walked through it on dry ground.


## Psalms 78:14 (id: 10019078014)

14He led them by a bright cloud during the day and by a fiery light during the night.


## Psalms 78:15 (id: 10019078015)

15He split rocks open in the wilderness and gave to our ancestors plenty of water from deep inside the earth.


## Psalms 78:16 (id: 10019078016)

16He caused a stream of water to flow from the rock; the water flowed like a river.


## Psalms 78:17 (id: 10019078017)

17But our ancestors continued to sin against God; in the desert they rebelled against the one who is greater than any other spirit being.


## Psalms 78:18 (id: 10019078018)

18By demanding that God give them the food that they desired, they tried to find out if he would always do what they requested him to do.


## Psalms 78:19 (id: 10019078019)

19They insulted God by saying, “God surely cannot supply food for us here in this desert.


## Psalms 78:20 (id: 10019078020)

20It is surely true that Moses struck the rock, and God made a huge amount of water gush out, but we, his people, {doubt} that he can also provide bread and meat for us.”


## Psalms 78:21 (id: 10019078021)

21So when Yahweh heard that, he became very angry, and he sent a fire to burn up some of his Israelite people.


## Psalms 78:22 (id: 10019078022)

22He did that because they did not trust in him, and they did not believe that he would rescue them.


## Psalms 78:23 (id: 10019078023)

23But God spoke to the sky above them and commanded it to open like a door,


## Psalms 78:24 (id: 10019078024)

24and then food fell down like rain, food that they named “manna.” God gave them grain from heaven.


## Psalms 78:25 (id: 10019078025)

25So the people ate the food that angels eat. God gave to them all the manna that they wanted.


## Psalms 78:26 (id: 10019078026)

26Later, he caused the wind to blow from the east, and by his power he also sent wind from the south.


## Psalms 78:27 (id: 10019078027)

27The wind brought birds to eat which fell like rain. They were as uncountable as the grains of sand on the seashore.


## Psalms 78:28 (id: 10019078028)

28God caused those birds to fall in the middle of their camp. There were birds all around their tents.


## Psalms 78:29 (id: 10019078029)

29So the people {cooked the birds and} ate the meat; their stomachs were full because God had given them what they wanted.


## Psalms 78:30 (id: 10019078030)

30But they had not yet eaten all that they wanted,


## Psalms 78:31 (id: 10019078031)

31when God angrily killed some of their strongest men. He even sent young Israelite men to their graves.


## Psalms 78:32 (id: 10019078032)

32In spite of all this, the people continued to sin; in spite of all the miracles that God had performed, they still did not trust that he would take care of them.


## Psalms 78:33 (id: 10019078033)

33So he shortened their lives so they seemed to only be a few days or like they were gone as quickly as a breath of air, and he terrified them during even those few years.


## Psalms 78:34 (id: 10019078034)

34Whenever God caused some of the Israelites to die, the others would repent; they would be sorry and seriously ask God to save them.


## Psalms 78:35 (id: 10019078035)

35They would remember that God was like a huge rock on which they would be safe, and that he, who was greater than any other spirit being, was the one who would buy them back.


## Psalms 78:36 (id: 10019078036)

36But they tried to trick God by lying to him.


## Psalms 78:37 (id: 10019078037)

37They were not loyal to him; they did not keep the promises between God and them.


## Psalms 78:38 (id: 10019078038)

38But God acted mercifully toward his people. He forgave them for having sinned and did not get rid of them. Many times he decided not to become angry with them and did not furiously punish them.


## Psalms 78:39 (id: 10019078039)

39He remembered that they were only humans who die, humans who disappear as quickly as a wind that blows by and then is gone.


## Psalms 78:40 (id: 10019078040)

40Many times our ancestors rebelled against God in the wilderness and made him very sad.


## Psalms 78:41 (id: 10019078041)

41Many times they did evil things to find out if they could do those things without God punishing them. They frequently caused the holy God whom the Israelites worship to become sad.


## Psalms 78:42 (id: 10019078042)

42They did not remember how powerfully he had acted, and they {forgot} about the time when he rescued them from their enemies.


## Psalms 78:43 (id: 10019078043)

43They forgot about all the wonderfully powerful things he did near Zoan in Egypt.


## Psalms 78:44 (id: 10019078044)

44He turned the rivers of {Egypt} to blood, so that the people could not drink their streams.


## Psalms 78:45 (id: 10019078045)

45He sent swarms of flies among the people of Egypt that bit them, and he sent frogs that destroyed everything.


## Psalms 78:46 (id: 10019078046)

46He gave their crops to the locusts. He let swarming locusts eat everything they had worked for.


## Psalms 78:47 (id: 10019078047)

47He killed their grape vines with hail, and killed their sycamore trees with frost.


## Psalms 78:48 (id: 10019078048)

48He sent a terrible thunderstorm with hail to kill all their domestic animals.


## Psalms 78:49 (id: 10019078049)

49Because God was fiercely angry with the people of Egypt, he caused them to be very distressed. The disasters that struck them were like a group of angels that destroyed everything.


## Psalms 78:50 (id: 10019078050)

50He let himself be angry with them, and he did not prevent them from dying; he sent a serious illness that killed many of them.


## Psalms 78:51 (id: 10019078051)

51In that plague he caused all the firstborn sons of the people of Egypt to die.


## Psalms 78:52 (id: 10019078052)

52Then he led his people out of Egypt like a shepherd leads his sheep, and he guided them like a flock in the desert.


## Psalms 78:53 (id: 10019078053)

53He led them safely, and they were not afraid, but their enemies drowned in the sea.


## Psalms 78:54 (id: 10019078054)

54Later God used his power to bring the Israelite people to the land he had set apart, to this hill country that he had taken for himself.


## Psalms 78:55 (id: 10019078055)

55He drove out the people groups as the Israelites advanced; he distributed the land for each tribe to possess as their own, and he settled the tribes of Israel in the homes of those peoples.


## Psalms 78:56 (id: 10019078056)

56But the Israelites defied the God who is greater than all other spirit beings and disobeyed him to see what he would do. They did not obey what he had required them to do.


## Psalms 78:57 (id: 10019078057)

57They turned away and were unfaithful, just as their ancestors had been; they were as unreliable as a bow that does not shoot straight.


## Psalms 78:58 (id: 10019078058)

58They made God angry by worshiping at shrines on the hilltops, and they made him jealous by worshiping carved idols.


## Psalms 78:59 (id: 10019078059)

59When God saw what they were doing, he became very angry and completely rejected the Israelite people.


## Psalms 78:60 (id: 10019078060)

60He stopped living at Shiloh, the place where he had set up his tent and lived among people.


## Psalms 78:61 (id: 10019078061)

61He let enemies capture the sacred chest which showed how strong and glorious he was.


## Psalms 78:62 (id: 10019078062)

62He let enemies kill his people in battle, because he was very angry with the people who belonged to him.


## Psalms 78:63 (id: 10019078063)

63Fire destroyed the young men, and no one sang wedding songs for the young women.


## Psalms 78:64 (id: 10019078064)

64Enemies killed the priests in battle, and the wives they left behind were not able to mourn for them.


## Psalms 78:65 (id: 10019078065)

65Then the Lord acted as though he had been sleeping and had woken up, like a warrior who becomes alert after drinking wine.


## Psalms 78:66 (id: 10019078066)

66He drove his enemies back and disgraced them forever.


## Psalms 78:67 (id: 10019078067)

67He did not choose the descendants of Joseph to be the leading tribe, and he did not choose the tribe of Ephraim.


## Psalms 78:68 (id: 10019078068)

68Instead he chose the tribe of Judah whose capital is on Mount Zion, which he loves.


## Psalms 78:69 (id: 10019078069)

69He had them build his temple there, high up like the heavens, and he established it to last as long as the earth.


## Psalms 78:70 (id: 10019078070)

70He chose David, who served him, and took him away from tending the sheep.


## Psalms 78:71 (id: 10019078071)

71He took David from caring for the mother sheep and their lambs and appointed him to lead the people of Israel, the people who belonged to God.


## Psalms 78:72 (id: 10019078072)

72David led them with a sincere and devoted heart, and he guided them with great skill.


## Psalms 79:1 (id: 10019079001)

1A Levite from the clan of Asaph wrote this song. God, foreign nations have invaded the land that belongs to you. They have made your holy temple unfit for worship, and they have made Jerusalem a pile of ruins.


## Psalms 79:2 (id: 10019079002)

2They left the dead bodies of the people who were faithful to you for birds and wild animals to eat.


## Psalms 79:3 (id: 10019079003)

3They killed so many of your people that blood flowed like water all around Jerusalem. There were not enough people still alive to bury them.


## Psalms 79:4 (id: 10019079004)

4The people of the nations around us insult us and laugh at us.


## Psalms 79:5 (id: 10019079005)

5Yahweh, please do not stay so very angry with us, even though we sinned against you by worshiping other gods.


## Psalms 79:6 (id: 10019079006)

6The people of other nations do not honor you as God. You should be angry with them and punish them,


## Psalms 79:7 (id: 10019079007)

7because they are the ones who have killed so many Israelites and destroyed the land in which they lived.


## Psalms 79:8 (id: 10019079008)

8Please forgive the wicked things we did in the past. Quickly be merciful to us, because we are desperate.


## Psalms 79:9 (id: 10019079009)

9God, you are the one who saves us, so please help us! Do that for the sake of your glorious reputation. Forgive us for our sins so that people will honor you.


## Psalms 79:10 (id: 10019079010)

10Do not let the other nations say, “Their God was not able to help them!” We want to see you punish those nations for killing the people who serve you.


## Psalms 79:11 (id: 10019079011)

11Listen to the moans of your people who are now captives, and use your great power to save those who are about to die.


## Psalms 79:12 (id: 10019079012)

12Lord, pay back our neighbors directly, seven times over, for the insults they have spoken against you!


## Psalms 79:13 (id: 10019079013)

13Then we will always thank you. You care for us, your people, the way a shepherd cares for his sheep. Each generation of Israelites will tell their children how wonderful you are.


## Psalms 80:0 (id: 10019080000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: The tune is “Lilies.” A Levite from the clan of Asaph wrote this song as an appeal to Yahweh for help.


## Psalms 80:1 (id: 10019080001)

1God, you are like a shepherd to the people of Israel, so please listen to me. You lead the descendants of Joseph the way a shepherd leads his flock. You were present with us in your temple above the statues of the cherubim; reveal your glory to us again!


## Psalms 80:2 (id: 10019080002)

2Just as you moved powerfully through the wilderness right ahead of the tribes of Ephraim, Benjamin, and Manasseh, come powerfully once again and save us.


## Psalms 80:3 (id: 10019080003)

3God, make us strong again. Regard us favorably and save us.


## Psalms 80:4 (id: 10019080004)

4Yahweh, you are the God who commands the heavenly armies. You have been angry with us, and so you have not been doing what we ask when we pray to you. But please now answer our prayers.


## Psalms 80:5 (id: 10019080005)

5You have caused us to experience constant sorrow. It feels as if you are punishing us three times as much as we deserve.


## Psalms 80:6 (id: 10019080006)

6The nations around us have become hostile to us, and our enemies make fun of us.


## Psalms 80:7 (id: 10019080007)

7God, you who command the heavenly armies, make us strong again! Regard us favorably and save us.


## Psalms 80:8 (id: 10019080008)

8You brought your people out of Egypt as if you were transplanting a vine from there. You forced other nations to leave this land and you allowed your people to settle in it, as if you were planting that vine here.


## Psalms 80:9 (id: 10019080009)

9You removed the foreign nations, just as a farmer clears the ground for a new plant. This enabled your people to become a strong nation that occupied the entire country.


## Psalms 80:10 (id: 10019080010)

10It was as if that vine became so large that its shadow darkened the mountains and its branches were like great cedar trees.


## Psalms 80:11 (id: 10019080011)

11They controlled the territory from the Great Sea in the west to the Euphrates River in the east.


## Psalms 80:12 (id: 10019080012)

12But now you have allowed enemies to invade and conquer us, as if you had torn down a fence that protected that vine, so that everyone who walked past it could take whatever fruit they wanted from it.


## Psalms 80:13 (id: 10019080013)

13Those enemies have destroyed the country, as if a wild boar had chewed up that vine and wild animals ate what remained.


## Psalms 80:14 (id: 10019080014)

14God, commander of the heavenly armies, please help us again! Look down from heaven and see what is happening to us! Come and care for your people, who are like a vine that you planted


## Psalms 80:15 (id: 10019080015)

15and worked hard to make sure that it would grow well. Your people are also like your own son whom you helped grow up to be strong.


## Psalms 80:16 (id: 10019080016)

16But now, because you do not regard them favorably, their enemies are destroying them as if someone had cut down that vine and burned it.


## Psalms 80:17 (id: 10019080017)

17Even though we are human, we are like your own son whom you helped grow up to be strong, so please help us with your power. Restore us to a position of honor.


## Psalms 80:18 (id: 10019080018)

18Then we will not worship other gods again. Give us new life, and we will worship only you.


## Psalms 80:19 (id: 10019080019)

19Yahweh, the God who commands the heavenly armies, make us strong again! Regard us favorably and save us.


## Psalms 81:0 (id: 10019081000)

0This is a note for the worship leader: Accompany the song with a stringed instrument. A Levite from the clan of Asaph wrote this song.


## Psalms 81:1 (id: 10019081001)
